[^p142-3]: **La.** Psalm. 33, 11. — Seq. locus est Ps. 110, 10: Initium sapientiae timor Domini (cfr. Prov. 1, 7. et Eccli. 1, 16.); tertius Sap. 7, 11: Venerunt autem mihi etc. — Inferius pro *materialium* M *temporalium.* L eodem sensu, brevius tamen, solvit arg.

     **En.** Ps. 33:11. — The following passage is Ps. 110:10: The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom (cf. Prov. 1:7 and Ecclus. 1:16); the third is Wisd. 7:11: Now all good things came to me etc. — Below, for *materialium* ("material") M has *temporalium* ("temporal"). L solves the argument in the same sense, but more briefly.

[^p142-4]: **La.** Psalm. 36, 25. — Glossam affert Petr. Lombard. (in hunc loc.); cfr. de ipsa August., Enarrat. in Ps. 36. serm. 3. n. 5. — De Glossa super Hebr. 13, 5. vide supra a. 1. ad 5. — Finem solut. I sic habet: *sed magis ad eam invitat.* L alterum solutionis modum omittit.

     **En.** Ps. 36:25. — Peter Lombard brings forward the Gloss (on this passage); on it cf. Augustine, *Enarrationes in Psalmos,* on Ps. 36, sermon 3, n. 5. — On the Gloss on Heb. 13:5 see above, article 1, reply 5. — I has the end of the solution thus: *sed magis ad eam invitat* ("but rather invites to it"). L omits the second manner of solution.

[^p142-5]: **La.** Psalm. 108, 10: Nutantes transferantur filii eius et mendicent et eiiciantur de habitationibus suis.

     **En.** Ps. 108:10: Let his children be carried about vagabonds and beg, and let them be cast out of their dwellings.

[^p142-6]: **La.** Scilicet *interlinearem* apud Lyranum in Eccli. 40, 29. Cfr. Raban., in hunc loc.

     **En.** Namely the *interlinear* Gloss in Lyra on Ecclus. 40:29. Cf. Rabanus, on this passage.

[^p143-1]: **La.** Cap. 38. Vide supra pag. 127, nota 2. — Seq. locus habetur supra a. 1. fundam. 20.

     **En.** Ch. 38. See above, page 127, note 2. — The following passage is found above, article 1, fundamentum 20.

[^p143-2]: **La.** Vers. 33. — De verbis Glossae, quae inferius allegantur, cf. ipsam obiectionem.

     **En.** Verse 33. — On the words of the Gloss which are cited below, cf. the objection itself.

[^p143-3]: **La.** Cfr. I. Cor. 9, 6. — Superius pro *secundum bene esse* K *de bene esse.*

     **En.** Cf. I Cor. 9:6. — Above, for *secundum bene esse* ("according to well-being") K has *de bene esse* ("of well-being").

[^p143-4]: **La.** Ioan. 21, 3. — Glossa, quam exhibet Strabus ut *ordinariam* super v. 1, sumta est ex Beda, in v. 1.

     **En.** John 21:3. — The Gloss, which Strabo gives as the *ordinary* Gloss on v. 1, is taken from Bede, on v. 1.

[^p143-5]: **La.** Homil. 87. (alias 86.) in Ioan. n. 2. Respicitur illud Ioan. 7, 39: Nondum enim erat Spiritus datus etc. In fine alia versio: Ad artem suam redierant.

     **En.** Homily 87 (otherwise 86) on John, n. 2. Reference is made to that text of John 7:39: For as yet the Spirit was not given etc. At the end another version reads: They had returned to their trade.

[^p143-6]: **La.** Exod. 20, 17: Non concupisces domum proximi tui etc. — Subinde respicitur Gal. 6, 2: Alter alterius onera portate, et sic adimplebitis legem Christi. — Inferius pro *denegasse* I *prohibuisse* et pro *solatium misericordiae et pietatis fraternae* edd. corrupte *solatium ex pietatis et caritatis fine.*

     **En.** Exod. 20:17: Thou shalt not covet thy neighbour's house etc. — Thereupon reference is made to Gal. 6:2: Bear ye one another's burdens, and so you shall fulfil the law of Christ. — Below, for *denegasse* ("to have denied") I has *prohibuisse* ("to have forbidden"), and for *solatium misericordiae et pietatis fraternae* the editions have corruptly *solatium ex pietatis et caritatis fine.*

[^p143-7]: **La.** Vers. 17: Non quia quaero datum etc. — Verba August., XIII. Confess. c. 26. seq. n. 39. seqq., sunt: Pascuntur autem his escis qui laetantur eis, nec illi laetantur eis, *quorum Deus venter est* [Phil. 3, 19.]. Neque enim et in illis, qui praebent ista, ea quae dant *fructus* est, sed quo animo dant... *Datum* est res ipsa, quam dat qui impertitur haec necessaria, veluti est nummus, cibus, potus, vestimentum, tectum, adiutorium. *Fructus* autem bona et recta voluntas datoris est (n. 41.)... Cum homines idiotae atque infideles... suscipiunt corporaliter reficiendos... cum id, quare faciendum sit et quo pertineat, ignorent, nec illi istos pascunt, nec isti ab illis pascuntur; quia nec illi haec sancta et recta voluntate operantur, nec isti eorum datis, ubi fructum nondum vident, laetantur; inde quippe animus pascitur, unde laetatur (c. 27. n. 42.). — Pro *regratians eis de beneficio* I *ubi regratiatur eis de beneficio.*

     **En.** Verse 17: Not that I seek the gift etc. — Augustine's words, *Confessions* XIII, ch. 26 f., nn. 39 ff., are: But they are fed with these foods who are gladdened by them, and they are not gladdened by them *whose God is their belly* [Phil. 3:19]. For in those too who furnish these things, it is not what they give that is the *fruit,* but the mind with which they give... The *gift* is the thing itself which he gives who bestows these necessaries, such as money, food, drink, clothing, shelter, help. But the *fruit* is the good and right will of the giver (n. 41)... When ignorant and unbelieving men take in those who are to be refreshed in body... while they know not why it should be done or to what it tends, neither do these feed those nor are those fed by these; because neither do these do these things with a holy and right will, nor are those gladdened by what these give, where they do not yet see the fruit; for the mind is fed from that whereby it is gladdened (ch. 27, n. 42). — For *regratians eis de beneficio* ("giving them thanks for the benefit") I has *ubi regratiatur eis de beneficio* ("where he gives them thanks for the benefit").

[^p144-1]: **La.** Epist. I. Tim. 6, 9: Nam qui volunt... in tentationem... multa inutilia et nociva.

     **En.** Epistle I Tim. 6:9: For they that will... into temptation... many unprofitable and hurtful desires.

[^p144-2]: **La.** Edd. *Egenus est qui cupit saturari. Est.*

     **En.** The editions have *Egenus est qui cupit saturari. Est* ("He is needy who desires to be filled. He is").

[^p144-3]: **La.** Edd. ultimum membrum omittunt.

     **En.** The editions omit the last member.

[^p144-4]: **La.** Vers. 11. seq. Vulgata post *denuntiamus* addit *et obsecramus in Domino Iesu Christo.*

     **En.** Verse 11 f. After *denuntiamus* ("we charge") the Vulgate adds *et obsecramus in Domino Iesu Christo* ("and beseech in the Lord Jesus Christ").

[^p144-5]: **La.** Vers. 60: Sine, ut mortui sepeliant mortuos suos. Cfr. tamen Matth. 8, 22. — Verba, quae Ambrosio tribuuntur (vide eius Expos. in Luc. lib. VII. n. 34, ubi eadem sententia tangitur), inveniuntur in Glossa *ordinaria* in hunc loc. apud Lyranum; de qua Glossa cfr. Beda in hunc loc. — Seq. locus est Act. 6, 2.

     **En.** Verse 60: Let the dead bury their dead. Cf. however Matt. 8:22. — The words attributed to Ambrose (see his *Exposition on Luke,* book VII, n. 34, where the same thought is touched on) are found in the *ordinary* Gloss on this passage in Lyra; on which Gloss cf. Bede on this passage. — The following passage is Acts 6:2.

[^p144-6]: **La.** Ioan. 6, 27: Operamini non cibum, qui perit. — Inferius pro *geminus* Maurini recte *genuinus* [dens], et pro *corrector scriptorum* textus originalis *corrector vitiorum.*

     **En.** John 6:27: Labour not for the meat which perisheth. — Below, for *geminus* ("twofold") the Maurists rightly have *genuinus* [*dens*] ("a back tooth"), and for *corrector scriptorum* ("corrector of the copyists") the original text has *corrector vitiorum* ("corrector of faults").

[^p144-7]: **La.** Cap. 21. n. 25: Si saltem habebant... dispertiti sunt, et credenda est eorum infirmitas et ferenda.

     **En.** Ch. 21, n. 25: If at least they had... they distributed, and their weakness is to be believed and borne with.

[^p144-8]: **La.** Cap. 25. n. 33. Textus originalis *ubicumque etiam,* codd. et 1, 2, 3, 4 *ubique etiam* pro *undecumque etiam.*

     **En.** Ch. 25, n. 33. The original text has *ubicumque etiam* ("wherever also"), and the codices and editions 1, 2, 3, 4 have *ubique etiam* ("everywhere also") for *undecumque etiam* ("from wheresoever also").

[^p144-9]: **La.** Cap. 21. n. 24: Isti autem fratres nostri temere sibi arrogant, quantum existimo, quod eiusmodi habeant potestatem. Si enim evangelistae [cfr. Eph. 4, 11.] sunt, fateor, habent; si ministri altaris, dispensatores Sacramentorum [cfr. I. Cor. 4, 1.], bene sibi istam non arrogant, sed plane vindicant potestatem.

     **En.** Ch. 21, n. 24: But these brethren of ours rashly claim for themselves, as I judge, that they have such a power. For if they are evangelists [cf. Eph. 4:11], I grant it, they have it; if they are ministers of the altar, dispensers of the Sacraments [cf. I Cor. 4:1], they rightly do not claim this power for themselves, but plainly vindicate it.

[^p145-1]: **La.** Ita D E, in aliis *carne.* Inferius pro *sudandum* edd. *sudatum.* — L tres rationes pro solutione tamen alio modo affert.

     **En.** So D E; in the others *carne* ("in flesh"). Below, for *sudandum* ("must be sweated") the editions have *sudatum.* — L brings forward three reasons for the solution, but in another manner.

[^p145-2]: **La.** Cfr. supra fundam. 15.

     **En.** Cf. above, fundamentum 15.

[^p145-3]: **La.** Alias 91. n. 3. Ante *avarorum* versio Graecae homiliae omittit *et.* — Seq. locus est ibid. n. 4.

     **En.** Otherwise 91, n. 3. Before *avarorum* ("of the covetous") the version of the Greek homily omits *et.* — The following passage is ibid., n. 4.

[^p145-4]: **La.** Vers. 4. — Seq. locus est ibid. v. 11, ubi Vulgata verbo *praecipio* praemittit *ego* et omittit *tuam.* — Aliquanto inferius pro *obsequium* I *officium,* M *officium vel obsequium.*

     **En.** Verse 4. — The following passage is ibid. v. 11, where the Vulgate puts *ego* before the word *praecipio* and omits *tuam.* — Somewhat below, for *obsequium* ("service") I has *officium* ("duty"), M *officium vel obsequium.*

[^p145-5]: **La.** Post can. *Habebat Dominus* (17.), C. 12. q. 1, Gratianus haec addit: His omnibus auctoritatibus claret... licet habere quid proprium, quod si etc.

     **En.** After the canon *Habebat Dominus* (17), C. 12 q. 1, Gratian adds these words: From all these authorities it is clear... it is lawful to have anything of one's own; and if etc.

[^p145-6]: **La.** Cap. 17. X. libr. V. tit. 31.

     **En.** Ch. 17 of the Decretals, book V, title 31.

[^p145-7]: **La.** Ut dicit Innocent. III. in C. *Innotuit nobis* (20.), X. de Electione et electi potest. (lib. I. tit. 6.), et occurrit in Glossa super legem *Ille* (13.), § Tempestivum, D. Ad senatus consultum Trebell. (lib. 36. tit. 1.). — De gradibus parentelae cfr. IV. Sent. d. 40. q. 3.

     **En.** As Innocent III says in the chapter *Innotuit nobis* (20), Decretals, *On Election and the Power of the One Elected* (book I, title 6); and it occurs in the Gloss on the law *Ille* (13), § *Tempestivum,* Digest, *On the Trebellian Decree of the Senate* (book 36, title 1). — On the degrees of kinship cf. *Sentences* IV, d. 40, q. 3.

[^p146-1]: **La.** I *approbata.*

     **En.** I has *approbata* ("approved").

[^p146-2]: **La.** K M *providerent.*

     **En.** K M have *providerent* ("they were to provide").

[^p146-3]: **La.** Pro *circa nihil,* quod praebet K, alii codd. vel cum 1, 2, 3, 4 *nihil,* vel cum Vat. *in nihil.* Superius pro *simulantes* K *simulando,* et pro *onerosi* ex D E substituimus *otiosi.*

     **En.** For *circa nihil* ("about nothing"), which K gives, the other codices have either, with editions 1, 2, 3, 4, *nihil,* or, with the Vatican edition, *in nihil.* Above, for *simulantes* ("feigning") K has *simulando* ("by feigning"), and for *onerosi* ("burdensome") we have substituted, from D E, *otiosi* ("idle").

[^p146-4]: **La.** Matth. 19, 21; cfr. supra pag. 125, nota 1, in qua etiam de Glossa, quae inferius allegatur. Hieron. in hunc locum ait: Et hoc ad perfectionem non sufficit, nisi post contemptas divitias Salvatorem sequatur [Glossa *interlinearis* addit *id est imitetur*], id est, relictis malis faciat bona... Sequitur autem Dominum qui imitator eius est et per vestigia illius graditur. — Pro *culmen* edd. *cumulum.*

     **En.** Matt. 19:21; cf. above, page 125, note 1, in which the Gloss cited below is also treated. Jerome on this passage says: And this does not suffice for perfection, unless, after riches have been despised, he follow the Saviour [the *interlinear* Gloss adds *id est imitetur,* "that is, imitate him"], that is, having left evils behind, let him do good things... Now he follows the Lord who is his imitator and walks in his footsteps. — For *culmen* ("summit") the editions have *cumulum* ("heap").

[^p146-5]: **La.** Vers. 8. — Inferius respicitur Luc. 16, 9. et Phil. 4, 15: In ratione dati et accepti. — Superius pro *veniens* M *proveniens.* Finem solut. exhibent edd. sic: *ut mutua diligentia vicissim possit impleri.*

     **En.** Verse 8. — Below, reference is made to Luke 16:9 and Phil. 4:15: In the matter of giving and receiving. — Above, for *veniens* ("coming") M has *proveniens* ("arising"). The editions give the end of the solution thus: *ut mutua diligentia vicissim possit impleri* ("that mutual diligence may in turn be fulfilled").

[^p146-6]: **La.** Epist. I. Cor. 12, 21: Non potest autem oculus dicere manui: opera tua non indigeo.

     **En.** Epistle I Cor. 12:21: And the eye cannot say to the hand: I need not thy help.

[^p147-1]: **La.** Vers. 17. — Glossa, quae est *ordinaria* apud Strabum et Lyranum, est secundum August., II. de Sermone Domini in monte, c. 16. n. 54. seq. Allegatur in ipsa Matth. 6, 33: Quaerite ergo primum regnum Dei etc.

     **En.** Verse 17. — The Gloss, which is the *ordinary* one in Strabo and Lyra, is according to Augustine, *On the Lord's Sermon on the Mount* II, ch. 16, n. 54 f. In it Matt. 6:33 is cited: Seek ye therefore first the kingdom of God etc.

[^p147-2]: **La.** Mich. 3, 11: Sacerdotes eius in mercede docebant, et prophetae eius in pecunia divinabant. — De Giezi et Balaam cfr. IV. Reg. 5, 20. seqq. et Num. 22, 7. seqq. — Pro *amavit* M *amaverunt.*

     **En.** Mich. 3:11: Her priests taught for hire, and her prophets divined for money. — On Giezi and Balaam cf. IV Kings 5:20 ff. and Num. 22:7 ff. — For *amavit* ("he loved") M has *amaverunt* ("they loved").

[^p147-3]: **La.** Vers. 7. — Glossa, quae est ex Beda (in hunc loc.), exhibetur a Strabo et Lyrano ut *ordinaria.*

     **En.** Verse 7. — The Gloss, which is from Bede (on this passage), is given by Strabo and Lyra as the *ordinary* one.

[^p147-4]: **La.** K *de qua.* Mox pro *occurrunt* I *occurrerent.*

     **En.** K has *de qua* ("concerning which"). Presently, for *occurrunt* ("occur") I has *occurrerent* ("would occur").

[^p147-5]: **La.** Scilicet ordinis Praedicatorum et Minorum. — Inferius respicitur Luc. 10, 2: Rogate ergo dominum messis etc. — Vat. *modernum ordinem.* I legit *moderni praedicatores.* Paulo ante pro *vel simpliciter* I *vel in toto.* Inferius pro *praedicaverunt* I M *praedicant.*

     **En.** Namely of the order of Preachers and of Minors. — Below, reference is made to Luke 10:2: Pray ye therefore the Lord of the harvest etc. — The Vatican edition has *modernum ordinem* ("the modern order"). I reads *moderni praedicatores* ("modern preachers"). A little before, for *vel simpliciter* ("or without qualification") I has *vel in toto* ("or in whole"). Below, for *praedicaverunt* ("they have preached") I M have *praedicant* ("they preach").

[^p147-6]: **La.** Cfr. Ioan. 12, 6. et 13, 29.

     **En.** Cf. John 12:6 and 13:29.

[^p147-7]: **La.** Vers. 1. — Glossa (ex Beda in hunc loc.) *ordinaria* apud Strabum et Lyranum: Sicut in Apostolis est forma episcoporum, sic in septuaginta duobus forma est presbyterorum.

     **En.** Verse 1. — The *ordinary* Gloss (from Bede on this passage) in Strabo and Lyra: As in the Apostles there is the form of bishops, so in the seventy-two there is the form of priests.

[^p147-8]: **La.** Vide tom. IV. pag. 535, nota 2. verba Bernardi, et infra q. 4. a. 3.

     **En.** See tome IV, page 535, note 2, the words of Bernard, and below, question 4, article 3.

[^p147-9]: **La.** Vers. 6. — Glossam, quae est secundum August., de Opere monachor. c. 15. n. 16, exhibet Petr. Lombard. in hunc loc. — Inferius respicitur Matth. 11, 29: Discite a me, quia mitis sum et humilis corde. — Circa finem solut., posito *efficeretur* pro *aedificent,* edd. post *verum etiam verbo* prosequuntur: *humilitatis. Quomodo ergo* etc.

     **En.** Verse 6. — The Gloss, which is according to Augustine, *On the Work of Monks,* ch. 15, n. 16, is given by Peter Lombard on this passage. — Below, reference is made to Matt. 11:29: Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart. — Towards the end of the solution, having put *efficeretur* for *aedificent,* the editions continue after *verum etiam verbo* thus: *humilitatis. Quomodo ergo* etc.

[^p148-1]: **La.** I *miris,* ed. Alex. Hal. *nimis abundantes.* Nulla lectio placet.

     **En.** Codex I *miris;* the edition of Alexander of Hales, *nimis abundantes.* Neither reading is satisfactory.

[^p148-2]: **La.** Can. 2, sumtus ex Hieron., I. adversus Iovinian. n. 18. — Inferius edd. cum M omittunt *ab esu.*

     **En.** Canon 2, taken from Jerome, *Against Jovinian* I, n. 18. — Below, the editions with M omit *ab esu.*

[^p148-3]: **La.** Epist. II. Thess. 2, 2. — Immediate post respicitur Matth. 23, 5, de quo vide supra pag. 140, nota 5; subinde Luc. 16, 15: Vos estis, qui iustificatis vos coram hominibus.

     **En.** The letter II Thess. 2:2. — Immediately after, Matt. 23:5 is had in view, on which see above, p. 140, note 5; then Luke 16:15: You are they who justify yourselves before men.

[^p148-4]: **La.** Cap. 14. In fine pro *claruit* textus originalis *inclaruit.*

     **En.** Chapter 14. At the end, for *claruit* the original text has *inclaruit.*

[^p148-5]: **La.** Libr. contra Vigilant. n. 13. Allegatur Gal. 2, 9. seq. et I. Cor. 16, 2. In fine respicitur Ps. 1, 2: In lege eius meditabitur die ac nocte (cfr. Iosue 1, 8.); Num. 18, 20: Dixitque Dominus ad Aaron: In terra eorum nihil possidebitis nec habebitis partem inter eos, ego pars et hereditas tua etc. (cfr. Deut. 18, 1. seq.); II. Cor. 8, 13. seq.: Non enim, ut aliis sit remissio, vobis autem tribulatio, sed ex aequalitate. In praesenti tempore vestra abundantia illorum inopiam suppleat etc. — Textus originalis ante *ecclesiis gentium* omittit *in,* et post *solatia dirigantur* plura addit. — D paulo superius post *mendicatione,* omissis subnexis, solutionem obiectionis concludit. — L in fine suae solut. haec bene observanda addit: Hanc quaestionem determinavit ita magister Bonaventura de Ordine Fratrum Minorum, secundum quod a quodam clerico potuit reportari. Deo gratias et Amen. — Porro in eodem codice L ad latus *fundamentorum, responsionis* et *solutionis* obiectorum scriptae sunt animadversiones contra doctrinam S. Bonaventurae satis acriter exhibitae, et *praecise eaedem,* quas S. Doctor in sequenti *replicatione* recenset et magna vi rationum et eloquentiae refellit. Animadversiones istae certo sunt vel Gulielmi a Sancto Amore, vel sectatorum eiusdem. De ipsis diffuse agitur in nostris Prolegomenis ad hanc quaestionem.

     **En.** The book against Vigilantius, n. 13. Gal. 2:9 ff. and I Cor. 16:2 are alleged. At the end Ps. 1:2 is had in view: On his law he shall meditate day and night (cf. Josue 1:8); Num. 18:20: And the Lord said to Aaron: You shall possess nothing in their land, neither shall you have a portion among them: I am thy portion and inheritance etc. (cf. Deut. 18:1 f.); II Cor. 8:13 f.: For I mean not that others should be eased, and you burdened, but by an equality. In this present time let your abundance supply their want etc. — The original text omits *in* before *ecclesiis gentium,* and after *solatia dirigantur* adds several things. — D, a little above, after *mendicatione,* omitting what follows, concludes the solution of the objection. — L, at the end of its own solution, adds these things, well worth observing: Thus did master Bonaventure of the Order of Friars Minor determine this question, according as it could be reported by a certain cleric. Thanks be to God, and Amen. — Further, in the same codex L, beside the *fundamenta,* the *response* and the *solution* of the objections, there are written animadversions against the doctrine of St. Bonaventure, set out sharply enough, and *precisely the same ones* which the holy Doctor reviews in the following *replication* and refutes with great force of reasoning and eloquence. Those animadversions are certainly either William of Saint-Amour's or his followers'. They are treated at length in our Prolegomena to this question.

[^p149-1]: **La.** Quae sequuntur exhibent solum D I M et edd., E K ipsa omittunt. Vide dicta in fine notae praecedentis. — Aliquanto inferius vocibus *post collectae* M interserit *me.*

     **En.** What follows is given only by D I M and the editions; E K omit it. See what was said at the end of the preceding note. — Somewhat lower, M inserts *me* among the words *post collectae.*

[^p149-2]: **La.** Cap. 15. X. lib. I. tit. 31: Quibus [praedicatoribus, visitatoribus, confessariis ab episcopis constitutis] ipsi, cum indiguerint, congrue necessaria subministrent, ne pro necessariorum defectu compellantur desistere ab incepto. — Superius ex M substituimus *pseudoprophetae* pro *pseudo.*

     **En.** Chapter 15, Decretals, book I, title 31: To whom [preachers, visitors and confessors appointed by the bishops] let them, when they are in need, duly supply what is necessary, lest for want of necessaries they be compelled to desist from what they have begun. — Above, from M, we have substituted *pseudoprophetae* for *pseudo.*

[^p149-3]: **La.** Vers. 1: Paulus Apostolus non ab hominibus neque per hominem, sed per Iesum Christum etc. — Seq. locus est Ioan. 20, 21. — Superius pro *apparentiam sophisticam* I *falsitatem.*

     **En.** Verse 1: Paul, an Apostle, not of men, neither by man, but by Jesus Christ etc. — The following passage is John 20:21. — Above, for *apparentiam sophisticam* codex I has *falsitatem.*

[^p149-4]: **La.** Vide notam penultimam.

     **En.** See the last note but one.

[^p149-5]: **La.** In solut. ad 11. — Inferius, post *Christi* M addito *electis,* prosequitur *Et quia missi* etc.

     **En.** In the solution to argument 11. — Below, after *Christi,* M, having added *electis,* continues *Et quia missi* etc.

[^p149-6]: **La.** Vers. 28: Et quosdam quidem posuit… deinde virtutes, exinde gratias curationum, opitulationes etc. — Glossam exhibet Petr. Lombard. (in hunc loc.), quae est apud Lyranum *interlinearis.*

     **En.** Verse 28: And God indeed hath set some… then miracles, then the graces of healing, helps etc. — Peter Lombard gives the Gloss (on this passage), which in Lyra is the *interlinear* one.

[^p150-1]: **La.** Psalm. 140, 4. — De subauctoritate cfr. I. Sent. d. 15. p. I. q. 3. ad 5. et q. 4. in corp. et ad 3.

     **En.** Psalm 140:4. — On sub-authority cf. I *Sent.* d. 15, pars I, q. 3, ad 5, and q. 4 in the body and ad 3.

[^p150-2]: **La.** In solut. ad 20. — D addit *positis.* Superius ante *mihi imponitur* substituimus *ab eis* pro *rabies,* quod corrupte habent codd. et edd., excepto I, qui verbum illud omittit; M et 5 *sicut rabies mihi imponit.* Inferius pro *et paupertatis altitudinem* I *in altitudine paupertatis.*

     **En.** In the solution to argument 20. — D adds *positis.* Above, before *mihi imponitur,* we have substituted *ab eis* for *rabies,* which the codices and the editions have corruptly, except I, which omits that word; M and edition 5, *sicut rabies mihi imponit.* Below, for *et paupertatis altitudinem* codex I has *in altitudine paupertatis.*

[^p150-3]: **La.** Cfr. Vita prima S. Francisci Assis., auctore B. Thoma de Celano, eius discipulo, c. 9, et Legenda trium sociorum, c. 8. Verba illa sunt Matth. 10, 9; Luc. 9, 3.

     **En.** Cf. the First Life of St. Francis of Assisi, by blessed Thomas of Celano, his disciple, c. 9, and the Legend of the Three Companions, c. 8. Those words are Matt. 10:9; Luke 9:3.

[^p150-4]: **La.** Epist. III. v. 8. seq.: Nos ergo debemus suscipere huiusmodi, ut cooperatores simus veritatis… sed is qui amat primatum gerere in eis, Diotrephes, non recipit nos. — Glossa *ordinaria* apud Strabum et Lyranum: Diotrephes non curat nostram auctoritatem. Diotrephes, haeresiarcha illius temporis, nova docendo principatum sibi usurpabat (secundum Bedam, in hunc loc.).

     **En.** The third letter, v. 8 f.: We therefore ought to receive such, that we may be fellow-helpers of the truth… but he who loveth to have the pre-eminence among them, Diotrephes, doth not receive us. — The *ordinary* Gloss in Strabo and Lyra: Diotrephes cares nothing for our authority. Diotrephes, a heresiarch of that time, was usurping the primacy for himself by teaching new things (according to Bede, on this passage).

[^p150-5]: **La.** Cfr. supra fundam. 9-13. — Inferius respicitur Regula iuris in VI. regul. 28: Quae a iure communi exorbitant nequaquam ad consequentiam sunt trahenda. — Eadem locutione utitur adversarius in praedicto codice L circa finem responsionis, negans ipsam conclusionem principalem.

     **En.** Cf. above, fundamenta 9-13. — Below, the Rule of law in the Sixth Book, rule 28, is had in view: Things that depart from the common law are by no means to be drawn into a precedent. — The adversary uses the same expression in the aforesaid codex L near the end of his response, denying the principal conclusion itself.

[^p150-6]: **La.** Scilicet in fundam. 9. seq. — Seq. locus est Ps. 69, 6. Glossa, quae est secundum Cassiodor. (in hunc loc.), apud Strabum et Lyranum est *ordinaria.* Allegatur in ipsa v. 3. eiusdem Psalmi.

     **En.** Namely in fundamentum 9 ff. — The following passage is Ps. 69:6. The Gloss, which is according to Cassiodorus (on this passage), in Strabo and Lyra is the *ordinary* one. In it v. 3 of the same Psalm is alleged.

[^p150-7]: **La.** Cfr. Rom. 8, 34; Hebr. 7, 25, et I. Ioan. 2, 1. — Inferius substituimus *textus hoc* pro *textus hic.*

     **En.** Cf. Rom. 8:34; Heb. 7:25, and I John 2:1. — Below, we have substituted *textus hoc* for *textus hic.*

[^p150-8]: **La.** Matth. 8, 20. et Luc. 9, 58: Filius autem hominis non habet, ubi caput reclinet. — Mox pro *cum haberet* I *si haberet.*

     **En.** Matt. 8:20 and Luke 9:58: But the Son of man hath not where to lay his head. — Presently, for *cum haberet* codex I has *si haberet.*

[^p151-1]: **La.** Vers. 29.

     **En.** Verse 29.

[^p151-2]: **La.** Ut narrat Abdias supra pag. 138, nota 5. allegatus. — Ed. Alex. Hal. prosequitur: *duobus potentibus mendicare et egere nolentibus, qui conversi fuerant retro, qui postmodum poenitentes mendicitatem* etc.; et infra *ex paupertate humili* pro *ex paupertate et humilitate.* Immediate post pro *et voluntaria* M *et voluntate.*

     **En.** As Abdias relates, cited above on p. 138, note 5. — The edition of Alexander of Hales continues: *for two powerful men unwilling to beg and to be in need, who had turned back, who afterwards, repenting, mendicancy* etc.; and below *ex paupertate humili* for *ex paupertate et humilitate.* Immediately after, for *et voluntaria* M has *et voluntate.*

[^p151-3]: **La.** Matth. 19, 21: Vade, vende etc. — Seq. locus est II. Cor. 8, 9: Si enim gratiam etc.; tertius ibid. v. 10: Et consilium in hoc do. — De Glossa cfr. supra pag. 126, nota 2.

     **En.** Matt. 19:21: Go, sell etc. — The following passage is II Cor. 8:9: For if the grace etc.; the third, ibid. v. 10: And herein I give counsel. — On the Gloss cf. above, p. 126, note 2.

[^p151-4]: **La.** Matth. 19, 12. — Verba August. sunt in Ioan. Evang. tr. 15. n. 2.: In omni re, quam gessit etc. — Sententia altera videtur expressa in Regula monachorum etc. (inter opera Hieron.) c. 19. (de humilitate Christi, quem imitari debemus): Imitemur ergo Salvatorem nostrum, quia ipse mitis et humilis… Amemus et nos Christum… Quodque beata Dei genitrix a Filio suo non didicit, caelos fabricare, Angelos creare, mira Deitatis insignia operari, sed tantum humiliari ceteraque virtutum documenta intra humilitatis custodiam aggregare. Cfr. August., Serm. 69. (alias 10. de Verbis Domini), c. 1. n. 2: Discite a me non mundum fabricare… sed *quoniam mitis sum et humilis corde* [Matth. 11, 29.].

     **En.** Matt. 19:12. — Augustine's words are in the Tractates on John's Gospel, tr. 15, n. 2: In every thing which he did etc. — The other opinion seems expressed in the Rule of monks etc. (among the works of Jerome), c. 19 (on the humility of Christ, whom we ought to imitate): Let us therefore imitate our Saviour, because he himself is meek and humble… Let us too love Christ… And what the blessed Mother of God did not learn from her Son was to build the heavens, to create Angels, to work the wondrous marks of the Godhead, but only to be humbled and to gather the rest of the lessons of the virtues within the guard of humility. Cf. Augustine, Sermon 69 (elsewhere 10, On the Words of the Lord), c. 1, n. 2: Learn of me, not to build the world… but *because I am meek and humble of heart* [Matt. 11:29].

[^p151-5]: **La.** Cap. 16. n. 31. seq. Cfr. supra pag. 118, nota 5.

     **En.** Chapter 16, n. 31 f. Cf. above, p. 118, note 5.

[^p151-6]: **La.** I M et ed. Alex. Hal. *funditus subvertitur.*

     **En.** Codices I M and the edition of Alexander of Hales, *funditus subvertitur.*

[^p151-7]: **La.** Vide supra fundam. 20.

     **En.** See above, fundamentum 20.

[^p151-8]: **La.** I et ed. Alex. Hal. addit *et intelligenti.* Superius pro *pari modo* I *pari ratione.*

     **En.** Codex I and the edition of Alexander of Hales add *et intelligenti.* Above, for *pari modo* codex I has *pari ratione.*

[^p151-9]: **La.** Fundam. 16. seqq.

     **En.** Fundamenta 16 ff.

[^p152-1]: **La.** I legit: *caritatis servo Dei humiliter et caritative serviebat, et sanctus Benedictus, quia humiliter et gratanter pro Christo suscipiebat.*

     **En.** Codex I reads: *caritatis servo Dei humiliter et caritative serviebat, et sanctus Benedictus, quia humiliter et gratanter pro Christo suscipiebat.*

[^p152-2]: **La.** Num. 11. Allegatur Ps. 83, 11. et Dan. 3, 12. seqq.

     **En.** Number 11. Ps. 83:11 and Dan. 3:12 ff. are alleged.

[^p152-3]: **La.** Rom. 13, 14: Carnis curam ne feceritis in desideriis. — Ex D M paulo inferius addidimus *etiam si se… videantur.*

     **En.** Rom. 13:14: Make not provision for the flesh in its concupiscences. — From D M we have added a little below *etiam si se… videantur.*

[^p152-4]: **La.** Respicitur I. Cor. 6, 15; seq. locus est Ps. 39, 18.

     **En.** I Cor. 6:15 is had in view; the following passage is Ps. 39:18.

[^p152-5]: **La.** Ex D et 1, 2, 3 supplevimus *ad.* Infra posuimus cum I et ed. Alex. Hal. *si imponeret* pro *si imponeretur.*

     **En.** From D and editions 1, 2, 3 we have supplied *ad.* Below we have set, with I and the edition of Alexander of Hales, *si imponeret* for *si imponeretur.*

[^p152-6]: **La.** I *revocabuntur.*

     **En.** Codex I, *revocabuntur.*

[^p152-7]: **La.** I legit: *panem furtim sublatum multiplicavit ad aliorum sustentationem, sicut audivimus, quod per mendicum multiplicavit ad solatium pauperum. Quodsi.* Idem etiam in seqq. ab aliis codd. recedit.

     **En.** Codex I reads: *panem furtim sublatum multiplicavit ad aliorum sustentationem, sicut audivimus, quod per mendicum multiplicavit ad solatium pauperum. Quodsi.* The same codex also departs from the others in what follows.

[^p152-8]: **La.** M addit *invenit.* Deinde pro *temerarie invitavit* I (cum verborum transpositione) *alios invitavit.*

     **En.** M adds *invenit.* Then, for *temerarie invitavit,* codex I (with a transposition of the words) has *alios invitavit.*

[^p152-9]: **La.** Ex D supplevimus *sed;* ed. Alex. Hal. *contrarium apparet,* et infra posuimus ex canone, I et ed. Alex. Hal. *revelat* pro *revelabat,* et cum 1, 2, 3, 4 *ut vadat* pro *quod vadat.*

     **En.** From D we have supplied *sed;* the edition of Alexander of Hales has *contrarium apparet,* and below we have set, from the canon, I and the edition of Alexander of Hales having *revelat* for *revelabat,* and with editions 1, 2, 3, 4 *ut vadat* for *quod vadat.*

[^p152-10]: **La.** Can. *Sacerdos ante omnia* (2.), de Poenit. dist. 6.

     **En.** The canon *Sacerdos ante omnia* (2), On Penance, distinction 6.

[^p153-1]: **La.** Art. 1. in corp. et hic ad 11, et quoad locum in regula supra fundam. 23.

     **En.** Article 1 in the body and here in the reply to argument 11, and as to the passage in the rule, above, fundamentum 23.

[^p153-2]: **La.** Ex D et ed. Alex. Hal. supplevimus *sic,* et inferius ante *tam sapiens* ex eodem cod. *hic.*

     **En.** From D and the edition of Alexander of Hales we have supplied *sic,* and below, before *tam sapiens,* from the same codex, *hic.*

[^p153-3]: **La.** S. Anselm., Lucens. episc. († 1086), inter capitula I. Collectionis canon. haec reconit: Ut prima Sedes a nullo iudicetur (18.), et: Ut nemo apostolicae Sedis iudicium iudicare, aut retractare praesumat (20.).

     **En.** St. Anselm, bishop of Lucca († 1086), among the chapters of book I of his Collection of canons, sets down these: That the first See be judged by no one (18), and: That no one presume to judge or to reconsider the judgement of the Apostolic See (20).

[^p153-4]: **La.** Psalm. 39, 18; 108, 17. Cfr. supra fundam. 9. seq.

     **En.** Psalm 39:18; 108:17. Cf. above, fundamentum 9 f.

[^p153-5]: **La.** Vers. 9. — Glossa in hunc locum est *ordinaria* apud Strabum et Lyranum et sumta est ex August., Serm. 36. (alias de Tempore 212.) c. 3. n. 4; ultima propos. ex Enarrat. in Ps. 101. serm. 1. n. 1. — Pro *expavescas* D I et ed. Alex. Hal. *erubescas,* refragante etiam originali.

     **En.** Verse 9. — The Gloss on this passage is the *ordinary* one in Strabo and Lyra, and is taken from Augustine, Sermon 36 (elsewhere On the Season 212), c. 3, n. 4; the last proposition from the Commentary on Ps. 101, sermon 1, n. 1. — For *expavescas,* D I and the edition of Alexander of Hales have *erubescas,* the original also being against it.

[^p153-6]: **La.** Alias 70. n. 4; sequens eiusdem locus refertur supra sub n. III. circa finem.

     **En.** Elsewhere 70, n. 4; the following passage of the same is reported above, under n. III, near the end.

[^p153-7]: **La.** Vers. 1. seqq.; alter locus est Gal. 2, 9. 10. [Vulgata: in gentes, ipsi in circumcisionem] cum Glossa *interlineari* apud Petr. Lombard. et Lyranum. — Post *pauperum* D addit secundum Glossam *in Ierusalem.*

     **En.** Verse 1 ff.; the other passage is Gal. 2:9-10 [the Vulgate: in gentes, ipsi in circumcisionem] with the *interlinear* Gloss in Peter Lombard and Lyra. — After *pauperum,* D adds, following the Gloss, *in Ierusalem.*

[^p154-1]: **La.** Cfr. supra arg. 6-9. ad oppos. — Inferius post *verbis meis, sed* supplendum est *dixi,* quod includitur in praecedentibus terminis.

     **En.** Cf. above, arguments 6-9 on the opposite side. — Below, after *verbis meis, sed,* one must supply *dixi,* which is contained in the preceding terms.

[^p154-2]: **La.** Epist. II. Thess. 3, 11. 12; subinde allegatur v. 6. — Glossa est apud Petrum Lombardum, Collectanea in Epist. S. Pauli, loc. cit.: Incipit… monens bonos, ut eos corripiant.

     **En.** The letter II Thess. 3:11-12; then v. 6 is alleged. — The Gloss is in Peter Lombard, Collectanea on the Letters of St. Paul, at the place cited: He begins… admonishing the good to correct them.

[^p154-3]: **La.** Vers. 10. 11: Rogamus autem vos, fratres, ut abundetis magis, et operam… quieti sitis, et ut vestrum negotium agatis et operemini… — Glossa est apud Lombard. ibid. — M et 1, 2, 3, 4 cum textu originali *ex otio* pro *de otio.*

     **En.** Verses 10-11: And we entreat you, brethren, that you abound more, and that you use your endeavour… to be quiet, and that you do your own business and work… — The Gloss is in Lombard, ibid. — M and editions 1, 2, 3, 4, with the original text, have *ex otio* for *de otio.*

[^p154-4]: **La.** Vers. 28. — Inferius posuimus *Glossae* pro *Glossa* fide M 1, 3, quia supra solut. ad 6. 9. et hic plures citantur Glossae.

     **En.** Verse 28. — Below we have set *Glossae* for *Glossa* on the faith of M and editions 1, 3, because above, in the solutions to arguments 6 and 9, and here, several Glosses are cited.

[^p154-5]: **La.** Cap. 25. n. 33, sed pluribus hic omissis. — In textu cum originali substituimus *tamen si et ipsi manibus* pro *sed manibus,* I M *si manibus,* D *cum omnia manibus.* Superius pro *qui repudiabant* M 1, 2, 3, 4 *qua repudiabant.*

     **En.** Chapter 25, n. 33, but with several things here omitted. — In the text we have substituted, with the original, *tamen si et ipsi manibus* for *sed manibus;* I M have *si manibus,* D *cum omnia manibus.* Above, for *qui repudiabant,* M and editions 1, 2, 3, 4 have *qua repudiabant.*

[^p154-6]: **La.** I M *fuerunt;* subinde post *labores* ex D I M supplevimus *erant.*

     **En.** I M, *fuerunt;* then, after *labores,* from D I M we have supplied *erant.*

[^p154-7]: **La.** I *humiliter;* infra ex D post *praemii* addidimus *aeterni;* denique post *vix credo* M supplet *quod.*

     **En.** Codex I, *humiliter;* below, from D, after *praemii* we have added *aeterni;* lastly, after *vix credo,* M supplies *quod.*

[^p154-8]: **La.** I praefigit *tamen.*

     **En.** Codex I prefixes *tamen.*

[^p154-9]: **La.** Ita I, in aliis *consilium,* et infra post *transigere non* ex D M supplevimus *est.*

     **En.** So codex I; in the others *consilium;* and below, after *transigere non,* from D M we have supplied *est.*

[^p155-1]: **La.** Matth. 19, 27; dein respicitur Ps. 9, 10, citatur Matth. 5, 3, et alluditur ad Sap. 5, 21: Et pugnabit cum illo orbis terrarum contra insensatos.

     **En.** Matt. 19:27; then Ps. 9:10 is had in view, Matt. 5:3 is cited, and there is an allusion to Wisd. 5:21: And the whole world shall fight with him against the unwise.

[^p155-2]: **La.** Vers. 36: Rogabat autem illum quidam de Pharisaeis, ut manducaret cum illo. — De mulieribus, *quae ministrabant ei de facultatibus suis,* cfr. Luc. 8, 3. — Substituimus *invitantibus* [quod Vat. habet in margine] loco *imitantibus;* hoc enim non congruit loco Lucae cit. Seq. locus est Act. 4, 34. 35. — Infra pro *communem pauperum* D non male *omnem pauperum.*

     **En.** Verse 36: And one of the Pharisees desired him to eat with him. — On the women *who ministered unto him of their substance,* cf. Luke 8:3. — We have substituted *invitantibus* [which the Vatican edition has in the margin] for *imitantibus;* for the latter does not suit the passage of Luke cited. The following passage is Acts 4:34-35. — Below, for *communem pauperum,* D has, not badly, *omnem pauperum.*

[^p155-3]: **La.** Ita M; in aliis omittitur *etiam* et *se.*

     **En.** So M; in the others *etiam* and *se* are omitted.

[^p155-4]: **La.** Cfr. supra solut. ad 7.

     **En.** Cf. above, the solution to argument 7.

[^p155-5]: **La.** Respicitur Matth. 23, 23. — Infra post *populi Christi* I *qui exemplis, documentis et consiliis animarum.* Subinde ante *missos* Vat. falso omittit *non.*

     **En.** Matt. 23:23 is had in view. — Below, after *populi Christi,* codex I has *qui exemplis, documentis et consiliis animarum.* Then, before *missos,* the Vatican edition falsely omits *non.*

[^p155-6]: **La.** Vide supra pag. 130, nota 1. 2. et in regula S. Francisci c. 9. 12.

     **En.** See above, p. 130, notes 1 and 2, and in the rule of St. Francis, cc. 9 and 12.

[^p155-7]: **La.** Supplevimus *ut,* exigente hoc ipsa grammaticali constructione. Inferius pro *est totius* Vat. *etiam totus;* eadem omittit *Et* ante *ideo.*

     **En.** We have supplied *ut,* the grammatical construction itself requiring it. Below, for *est totius,* the Vatican edition has *etiam totus;* the same omits *Et* before *ideo.*

[^p155-8]: **La.** Phil. 2, 10. — Hic expliciunt D I M; in edd. sequitur additamentum, quod novem auctoritates continet separatim positas. Cum autem harum tres iam in praecedentibus de paupertate quaestionibus a S. Doctore relatae sint, non videtur verisimile, illas ab eodem hic iterum esse repetitas aliasque adiunctas. Licet igitur hi loci occurrant etiam in antiquo codice E (qui totam immediate praecedentem replicationem omittit); tamen nobis convenientius esse visum est, ut hoc additamentum, cum videatur non genuinum esse et ad perfectionem quaestionis sit superfluum, ad calcem reiiceretur. Refertur autem praecipue secundum E, emendatis ope originalium nonnullis vitiis editionum et codicis.

     **En.** Phil. 2:10. — Here D I M come to an end; in the editions there follows an addition, which contains nine authorities set down separately. But since three of these have already been reported by the holy Doctor in the preceding questions on poverty, it does not seem likely that they were repeated here again by the same author and others joined to them. Although, therefore, these passages occur also in the ancient codex E (which omits the whole of the immediately preceding replication); nevertheless it has seemed to us more fitting that this addition, since it appears not to be genuine and is superfluous to the completeness of the question, should be relegated to the close. It is reported chiefly according to E, some faults of the editions and of the codex having been corrected by the help of the originals.



[^tr-vacare]: **En.** Translator's note: the Latin *vacare* holds together several senses that English has to split: being at leisure, being free, being free *for* something (with a dative, as here, *divinis vacant*) and being free *from* something (with *ab*). Here the dative governs, so the English takes the side of being free *for* divine things: the point of the Gloss is what these poor are given over to, not what they are released from. No *vaca-* word is rendered "rest" in this translation, because *quies* and *requies* take that word.

[^tr-vacare-2]: **En.** Translator's note: Augustine is punning, and the pun needs one English word in both halves. Here *vacare* has no dative of the thing one is free *for;* it stands absolute, with an ablative of the part of the body that is unoccupied, and so it leans to the other side of the word — plain leisure, which in these men is idleness. The English therefore takes "to be at leisure" in both halves: they want their hands at leisure, and Augustine wishes their tongues were at leisure too. Rendering the first half "idle" and the second "silent" would say what he means and lose the joke, which is his argument.

[^tr-vacare-3]: **En.** Translator's note: here *vacare* takes neither of the constructions the two notes above describe. It governs *ab* with the ablative — *ab ecclesiasticis occupationibus vacant* — and so it leans to the side of being free *from* something: what is in view is men on whom no church duty lies, who are therefore at liberty for manual work. The English takes "free from," keeping the same English root as the "free for" of the earlier passages, so that a reader can see it is one Latin word turning on its preposition. Bonaventure repeats Augustine's clause in his own voice a few lines later, and the same rendering stands there. As throughout, no *vaca-* word is rendered "rest."

## Notes

**Provenance, pp. 134–139.** Latin re-set from the IA djvu OCR (`raw/doctorisseraphic05bona_djvu.txt`, **raw L28999–L29851** for this span), with every heading, italic, apparatus anchor position, marginale and digit checked against the 450 dpi plates (`raw/vision/vol5/p-134..141.png`, 2571×3823 each, pixel size verified after extraction). Body raw usable throughout pp. 134–139; footers usable but digit-corrupt (below). **Italics come from the plate only.** Fresh literal English translation.

**Structure (pp. 134–139).** p. 134: below Art. I's replies 9 (tail)–10 (`bon-qpe-q2-a1`'s), `ARTICULUS II.` full measure, unanchored; its italic subtitle ***De paupertate quoad mendicitatem*¹** — **ANCHORED, n. 1** (Art. II's textual note: codd. A D E K M, and the L *reportatio*), read on a full-width crop: the only mark after `II.` is its point, and the numeral `1` stands after *mendicitatem* before the point. The opener (*Consequenter est quaestio de mendicitate…* — *mendicare* defined as *eleemosynam quaerere seu de quotidianis eleemosynis vivere*) asks whether to beg for Christ belongs to Christian perfection, *Et quod non, videtur*. **The opposing series comes FIRST here and argues AGAINST** (marginale `Ad oppositum.` beside arg. 1): **twenty arguments** — from authority 1–13 (Psalms, Ecclus., Acts, Luke, I–II Thess. with the Gloss; Gratian C. 12 q. 1 *Videntes*, *De consecratione* d. 1, the Code's law on able-bodied beggars), and from reason 14–20 (arg. 14 itself opens *Item, hoc ipsum ostenditur ratione*, numbered, marginale `Argg. ex ratione.`), ending with the secular masters' case against Dominic and Francis (arg. 20, *superstitiosus et vanus*). Then **`Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ: 1.`** on p. 136 (letter-spaced small caps in-column; marginale `Fundamenta ex auctoritate.`) — **thirty-seven arguments FOR mendicancy**: Scripture with its Glosses 1–8 (7 is Jerome on the Luke 16 text of 6); *Item, hoc ipsum ostenditur exemplo Christi* (arg. 9, numbered) 9–15; *ad hoc est exemplum Sanctorum* (arg. 16, numbered) 16–25 (Elias, John's disciples, Paul's disciples, Benedict, Alexius, Dominic, Francis, Gregory, Jerome); an UNNUMBERED run-head *Item, hoc ipsum ostenditur ratione.* (marginale `Rationes.`) before 26–37. ⭐ Quaracchi's labels are the reverse of Art. I's: there `Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ` carried the objections; here it carries the *fundamenta* and the objections stand first — **the replies' lemmata were checked one by one to learn which series they answer — see below.** ⭐ **p. 134 n. 1 explains the long italic L-variants in nearly every note of this span**: L is a clerical *reportatio* which Bonaventure's own *replicatio* calls *minus sufficienter … collectae*; Quaracchi prints its arguments *speciminis gratia*.

### ▶ RULINGS MET — STATED POSITIVELY (pp. 134–139)

- ⭐ ***vacare* — the dikaisune rule MET, and the translator's note WRITTEN.** Body: p. 137 `Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ` 8, the Gloss on II Cor. 8:14, *abundantia meritorum illorum spiritualium, **qui divinis vacant*** → ***"of those who are free for divine things"***, with the `tr-vacare` anchor after "things" (English only). Apparatus: p. 137 n. 2 (L's addition), *illi **qui vacant divinis** … pauperes mendicantes pro Christo **vacant divinis*** → *"those who are free for divine things … are free for divine things"* — **the same side (dative, free FOR), so no `tr-vacare-2`.** Latin `vaca-` census over pp. 134–139's body: one (*vacant*); apparatus: two. "rest": zero in the English.
- **Ruling 5 MET ONCE, rendering quoted:** p. 134 n. 1 `(in fundam. plerumque valde insufficienter)` → ***"(in the fundamenta, mostly very insufficiently)"*** — the plural, un-numbered form, meaning the L-reportatio's opening arguments.
- ***intellect-*:** p. 137 `Contra` 7 (Jerome) *intelligit super egenum* → "understandeth" (Douay Ps. 40:2), *nequaquam **intellectu*** → "not at all of understanding", *beatitudo est **intelligentiae*** → "the blessedness of intelligence"; *intelligitur* → "is understood" (pp. 137–138); p. 136 n. 3 *ad litteram intelligenda* → "to be understood according to the letter". **Zero bare "intellect".**
- **Ruling 2 not reached:** *lux* / *lumen* zero; p. 135 n. 5's *luminaria* (a church's lamps) → "lamps". ***pietas*** once → "piety" (p. 139 `Contra` 37). ***praeceptum*** → "precept" (p. 136 n. 3 *illa praecepta*; n. 4 *Istud praeceptum*), *praecipere* → "command"; ***consilium*** → "counsel" (`Contra` 6 *divinum consilium*, 31 *consilia implet*).

### ▶ REGISTER — the adopted choices as applied (pp. 134–139)

- ***mendicitas*** → "mendicancy" (subtitle, opener) · ***mendicatio*** → "begging" (`Contra` 23 *mendicationem suam*, 28, 30; p. 136 n. 3 *praecepit mendicationem*; p. 138 n. 10) · ***mendicare*** → "to beg" · ***mendicus*** → "beggar" · ***mendicantes*** → "mendicants" (arg. 9 *hoc est mendicantium* → "this is what mendicants do"; arg. 13 *lege de Mendicantibus validis* → "the law on able-bodied mendicants"; notes).
- ***egenus / egere / egestas*** → "needy / to be in need / need" · ***inops / inopia*** → "in want / want" (arg. 1 Ps. 33:10; `Contra` 8; `Contra` 10's Ps. 108 *hominem inopem* → "the man in want", Douay's "poor man" adjusted so *pauper* keeps "poor") · ***indigere / indigens*** → "to be indigent / indigent" (arg. 4 Ecclus. 40:29, Douay "indigent… want" adjusted to one root; p. 135 n. 4) · ***pauper*** → "poor".
- ***petere*** → "to request" · ***rogare*** → "to ask" · ***postulare*** → "to ask for / petition" · ***quaerere*** → "to seek" · ***appetere*** → "to crave" · ***desiderare*** → "to desire" (arg. 7 turns on *rogare vel petere* against *appetere*/*desiderare*, so each keeps its own verb). *accipere* / *recipere* → "receive".
- ***praelatus*** → "prelate" (`Contra` 2; p. 136 n. 4). ***abrenuntiare / renuntiare*** → "renounce". ***dominus*** (`Contra` 15 *sicut dominus*) → "lord".
- ***religio*** → "religious order" where it names an institute (arg. 12 *nec aliqua religio institui debet*; arg. 20 *in religionibus*), "religion" in *christiana religio* (notes). ***potestative / supplicative*** → "by right of (their) power / by supplication" (arg. 19; `Contra` 33). ***otium*** → "idleness" (not a *vaca-* word). ***vilis*** → "vile" (p. 136 n. 2; `Contra` 14 *vilius* → "viler").
- **Scripture** on the Douay base, adjusted where Quaracchi's Latin differs (Luke 9:48 without *istum* → "a child"; II Thess. 3:9 without *nosmetipsos*; II Cor. 8:14's second clause → "may be a supplement to your want", because the Gloss repeats the word).

I; `q1` printed *Solutio*)**. The *Respondeo* runs pp. 140–142 in one movement: **three ways of begging** — *ex necessitate naturae* (Lazarus; Gregory), *ex vitiositate culpae* (Ambrose *de Officiis;* Augustine *de Opere monachorum,* including the *vacare* pun), *ex supererogatione iustitiae* (begging *pro Christo imitando* and *pro Christo evangelizando*) — then the threefold debt (*multum · magis · maxime*), the long Gloss on Ps. 103:14, and an epilogue arguing that to condemn all mendicancy is to condemn the supreme Pontiff, Francis, Dominic, Alexius, Benedict, the Apostles, **Christ himself called *pauper* and *mendicus*,** and finally *universalis Ecclesiae status.* **The replies begin at p. 142's right column** and run **one per argument, in order, none combined**; **replies 1–19** fall in this span. ⭐ **The replies answer the FIRST series — the two proofs are recorded below.**

### ▶ RULINGS MET — STATED POSITIVELY (pp. 140–147)

- ⭐⭐ ***vacare* — six sites, five on the dative side and ONE on the other, which got the second translator's note.** The five: p. 140 *ut **vacet Deo** libera mente* → "that he may **be free for God** with a mind at liberty" · p. 141 (Augustine, Lenten sermon) *dum iugiter **Deo vacant*** → "while they are continually **free for God**" · p. 143 *tunc Apostoli **praedicationi** minime **vacabant*** → "the Apostles **were by no means free for preaching**" · p. 145 *spiritualibus **vacare** exercitiis et ecclesiasticae utilitati* → "**be free for** spiritual exercises and for the Church's benefit" · p. 146 *in qua **vacant divinis laudibus,** orationibus, vigiliis* → "in which they **are free for the divine praises,** for prayers, for vigils". **All five carry a DATIVE of the thing one is free FOR — the dative side exactly, so none of them takes a note.** ⛔ **The sixth is Augustine's pun at p. 140** (*de Opere monachorum* c. 22): *Utinam isti, qui **vacare volunt manibus,** omnino **vacarent et linguis**!* — **no dative at all; *vacare* stands absolute with an ablative of the unoccupied member,** so the sense leans to plain leisure (which in these men is idleness). Rendered **"who wish to be at leisure with their hands… were wholly at leisure with their tongues too"** — **ONE English root across both halves, as ruled** — with **`tr-vacare-2` anchored in the ENGLISH ONLY** after "hands". **"rest" appears nowhere in this span's English for a `vaca-` word.**
- **Ruling 5 MET TWICE, both renderings quoted:** p. 143 n. 1 `Seq. locus habetur supra a. 1. fundam. 20.` → ***"The following passage is found above, article 1, fundamentum 20."*** · p. 145 n. 2 `Cfr. supra fundam. 15.` → ***"Cf. above, fundamentum 15."*** — the singular numbered form both times, the Latin retained, as the apparatus convention requires. **Both are INTERNAL back-references into `bon-qpe-q2-a1`'s `Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ` series** (which that articulus's own register also calls *fundamenta*).
- ***intellect-*:** the stem occurs **fourteen times** in pp. 140–147's Latin (*intelligentiam, intelligitur* ×5, *intelligenda, intelligendum, male intelligentes, esse intelligendum, recte intelligimus, intelligi, intellecta*) and **every one goes to "understand(ing)"** — p. 140 *Ad praedictorum **intelligentiam*** → "For the **understanding** of what has been said"; p. 140 *male **intelligentes** Evangelium* → "**ill understanding** the Gospel"; p. 145 *si recte **intelligimus*** → "if we **understand** rightly". **Zero bare "intellect" over pp. 140–147**, and the narrow *intellectus agens* exception is not reached.
- **Ruling 2 not reached over pp. 140–147 either:** *lux* zero, *lumen* zero over pp. 140–147 (stem census on `lux|luc-|lumin`: only proper names — *Lucae, Lucam* — and p. 145's *fulserunt* "have shone", which is not a *lux* word). ***pietas*** → "piety" (p. 140 *provocet ad **pietatem***; p. 143 n. 6's *pietatis*). ***praeceptum/praecipere*** → "precept / command" (p. 144 *simpliciter esset praeceptum* → "were a precept without qualification"; p. 145 n. 4's *praecipio* → "I command"); ***consilium*** → "counsel" (p. 143 *illud est consilium… de bene esse consilii*; p. 146 *secundum divinum consilium*) — the *praeceptum*/*consilium* backbone is carried unchanged.

### ▶ REGISTER — the adopted choices as applied (pp. 140–147)

- ***mendicitas*** → "mendicancy" (p. 140 *mendicitas, etiam ex necessitate veniens*; p. 145 Deut. 15:4 *contra mendicitatem pauperum*; p. 147 n. 9's Gloss *non est mendicitas, sed potestas* → "is not mendicancy, but power") · ***mendicatio*** → "begging" (p. 140 the *Conclusio* itself; pp. 142, 144, 146) · ***mendicare*** → "to beg" · ***mendicus*** → "beggar" · ***mendicantes*** → "mendicants" only where it names the class (p. 146 *de lege de mendicantibus validis* → "the law on able-bodied mendicants", as on pp. 134–139), "those who beg" where it is a plain participle (p. 145 *qui debent pascere mendicantes*). **The pair is kept apart throughout.**
- ***vilificare se*** → "to make oneself vile" (p. 140 *ut se ipsum **vilificet** et humiliet* → "that he may **make himself vile** and humble himself"), kept apart from ***humiliare*** → "humble" and ***abiectio*** → "abjection" (p. 142 *usque ad **abiectionis** genus extremum*); p. 142's ***vilificatus*** → "**made vile**". `q1`'s choice applied without change, and p. 140 is the control the choice was made for — the two verbs stand side by side in one clause.
- ***egenus / egere / egestas*** → "needy / to be in need / neediness" · ***inops / inopia*** → "in want / want" (reply 1 turns on *duplex est inopia*) · ***indigere / indigentia*** → "to be indigent / indigence" (reply 4 turns on *indigentia spiritualis* against *corporalis*) · ***penuria*** → "scarcity" (p. 144, against *abundantia*) · ***saturitas*** → "satiety". **Five words, five English words, none shared.**
- ***petere*** → "to request" · ***rogare*** → "to ask" · ***quaerere*** → "to seek" · ***postulare*** → "to petition" · *accipere / recipere* → "receive" — the grid of pp. 134–139 unchanged, and it is load-bearing here: p. 142's *licitum est **accipere,** sed non est licitum **petere*** and p. 147's *accipere **potestative*** / *accipere **supplicative***.
- ***potestative / supplicative*** → "by right of (their) power / by supplication" (p. 147, reply 19's hinge) · ***subauctoritas*** → "sub-authority", ***subprincipalis*** → "sub-principal" · ***praelatus*** → "prelate" · ***religio*** → "religious order" where it names an institute · ***otium / otiositas*** → "idleness" (NOT a *vaca-* word; p. 140 *pro otio fovendo* → "for the fostering of idleness", p. 145's Gloss *otio deditus* → "given over to idleness") · ***proprium / proprietas*** → "property of one's own / property" (p. 145 reply 11's second defect) · ***dominium*** does not occur over pp. 140–147.
- **Scripture** on the Douay base, adjusted where Quaracchi's Latin differs: Luke 16:20–22 (*ulceribus plenus,* without *qui iacebat ad ianuam eius*); I Tim. 6:9 (*desideria multa et nociva* against Douay's "many unprofitable and hurtful desires" — Quaracchi's own note flags the variant); Deut. 15:4 *Non erit omnino indigens et mendicus inter vos* → "There shall be no one indigent and a beggar among you", so that *indigens* and *mendicus* keep the register's own words; I Cor. 9:19 *liber ex omnibus* → "free from all men" (so that "free" is not confused with the *vacare* sites, which are all "free FOR"); Matt. 19:21 *vade et vende omnia* (Quaracchi's shorter text).

### ⭐ PRINTING ANOMALIES — AS PRINTED, NOT EMENDED (pp. 140–147)

- **p. 141 *praedioatur*** (for *praedicatur*), in *A pluribus etiam praedioatur* — read at 3×: a round `o` where the `c` belongs, the bowl fully closed on both sides, at full ink strength with its neighbours. **An identifiable wrong sort, not a failing impression** → **as printed**; the English renders the sense ("it is also preached by more"). ⚠ Its two sister clauses in the same sentence print *praedicatur* correctly, which is the control.
- **p. 146 n. 5 *diligentia*** — in the editions' variant *ut mutua diligentia vicissim possit impleri:* the `g` inked as a hairline stem with no bowl and no descender, the flanking `i` and `e` at full strength. **Letters present minus strokes** → **transcribed plain** (the `bon-qmt-q4-a1` *sententia* precedent, as used for *usi* above), and disclosed here. The reading is determinate: *diligentia* is the only word the stem fits, and Quaracchi is quoting the editions against its own *indigentia* in the body.
- **p. 145 — the signature line `S. Bonav. — Tom. V.` stands under the LEFT register** (the same class as p. 137's, and like it not apparatus); **no gathering signature is visible on p. 145's right.**
- **p. 140 n. 5's Vulgate quotation prints *magnificant fimbrias* where the body's Augustine prints *fimbrias et phylacteria sua magnificant*** — the note and the body invert the pair. Both **as printed**; the note itself is Quaracchi telling the reader the source is Matt. 23:5, so the inversion is Augustine's, not a slip.
- **p. 142 *cum eo est*** (in the Gloss on Ps. 36:25, *quia verbum Dei semper cum eo est*) — the `o` of *eo* broken at the top right and reading as a `u` at 1×; at 3× the break is a chip in the bowl, the rest of the letter round. **Transcribed *eo*** (the syntax admits nothing else) and disclosed.
- **p. 147 n. 5 prints `Vat.` for the Vatican edition** — a siglum pp. 134–139 did not meet; expanded in the English only ("the Vatican edition"), the Latin left as printed.

**Provenance, pp. 148–155.** Latin re-set from the IA djvu OCR (`raw/doctorisseraphic05bona_djvu.txt`, **raw L30968–L32208** for this span), with every heading, italic, apparatus anchor position, marginale and digit checked against the 450 dpi plates (`raw/vision/vol5/p-148..156.png`, 2571×3823 each, pixel size verified after extraction). **Body raw usable throughout pp. 148–155; footers digit-corrupt as before.** *Italics come from the plate only.* Fresh literal English translation.

**Structure, pp. 148–155.** Reply 20 runs from p. 147's right column onto p. 148 (marginale `Triplex Dei dispositio.`), answering argument 20 exactly as the lemma predicted — *tutius et perfectius est sequi viros probatiores et sapientiores* — and closing the reply series at twenty, one per argument, in order, none combined. An unnumbered **epilogue** follows on p. 148 (marginale `Epilogus et confirmatio.`), gathering the whole and ending on Jerome *contra Vigilantium.* ⭐⭐ **Then a SECOND STRUCTURAL UNIT opens, and p. 134 n. 1 predicted it: `Rᴇᴘʟɪᴄᴀᴛɪᴏ ᴀᴅᴠᴇʀsᴜs ᴏʙɪᴇᴄᴛɪᴏɴᴇs ᴘᴏsᴛᴇᴀ ꜰᴀᴄᴛᴀs.`**, set in-column in letter-spaced small caps on p. 149's left column (**unanchored**; its opening sentence *His autem*¹ carries p. 149 n. 1). It runs pp. 149–155 and is built on **five censurae** the adversary is said to have made — the opener names them (*impertinentia · falsa · inconsequentia · dubia · doctrinae apostolicae inimica*) and the body then takes them in order as **I · II · III · IV · V**, each with numbered *Obi.* marginalia and a *Responsio.* The fifth (pp. 153–155) is the longest and closes with a recapitulation of the whole question on p. 154 (marginale `Epilogus totius quaestionis.`) and a final peroration on p. 155 ending **`Amen.`** ⛔ **So something DOES follow the replies, and it is exactly the *replicatio ad obiectiones denuo factas* that p. 134 n. 1 warned of** — the prediction was checked, not assumed, and p. 148 n. 5 and p. 155 n. 8 both name the unit by that word.

### ▶ RULINGS MET — STATED POSITIVELY (pp. 148–155)

- ⭐⭐⭐ ***vacare* — TWO NEW SITES, BOTH ON A THIRD SIDE OF THE WORD, AND THE THIRD TRANSLATOR'S NOTE WAS WRITTEN.** Both stand on p. 154 and both govern **`ab` with the ablative**: Augustine's *si corpore valent et **ab ecclesiasticis occupationibus vacant*** (quoted in the reply to the second objection of censura V) and Bonaventure's own repetition of the clause a few lines later, *qui **ab ecclesiasticis occupationibus vacant.*** **Neither existing note covers this construction** — `tr-vacare` is the dative "free FOR", `tr-vacare-2` the absolute "at leisure" of Augustine's pun — so **`tr-vacare-3` was written and anchored in the ENGLISH ONLY** after the first of the two, on the standing instruction that a third note is warranted only where the sense leans to a side the existing notes do not cover. Rendered **"are free from ecclesiastical occupations"** at both sites, deliberately keeping the same English root as the "free for" passages so that a reader can see one Latin word turning on its preposition. **"rest" appears nowhere in this span's English for a `vaca-` word.** Latin `vaca-` census over pp. 148–155: two, both in the body; apparatus: zero.
- **Ruling 5 MET SIX TIMES, every rendering quoted:** p. 150 n. 5 `Cfr. supra fundam. 9-13.` → ***"Cf. above, fundamenta 9-13."*** · p. 150 n. 6 `Scilicet in fundam. 9. seq.` → ***"Namely in fundamentum 9 ff."*** · p. 151 n. 7 `Vide supra fundam. 20.` → ***"See above, fundamentum 20."*** · p. 151 n. 9 `Fundam. 16. seqq.` → ***"Fundamenta 16 ff."*** · p. 153 n. 1 `…quoad locum in regula supra fundam. 23.` → ***"…as to the passage in the rule, above, fundamentum 23."*** · p. 153 n. 4 `Cfr. supra fundam. 9. seq.` → ***"Cf. above, fundamentum 9 f."*** **All six are INTERNAL back-references into `bon-qpe-q2-a1`'s `Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ` series.** Nine sites in the chunk in all.
- ***intellect-*:** the stem occurs three times over pp. 148–155 (*intelligat, intelligentiam, intelligere*) and **every one goes to "understand(ing)"** — p. 149 *si quis **intelligat** radicem potestatis* → "if one **understands** the root of the power"; p. 150 *ad **intelligentiam** veritatis* → "for the **understanding** of the truth". **Zero bare "intellect" in the chunk.**
- **Ruling 2 not reached here either:** *lux* zero, *lumen* zero over pp. 148–155 on a stem census. ***pietas*** → "piety" (p. 152's *ordinem caritatis et pietatis* → "the order of charity and piety"; p. 155's *humanus et pius*). ***praeceptum / praecipere*** → "precept / command" against ***consilium*** → "counsel" — the work's backbone, and it is load-bearing on p. 154, where *praecipit* recurs six times against *consultum a Domino ut perfectum* in the recapitulation.

### ▶ REGISTER — the adopted choices as applied (pp. 148–155)

- The whole *mendic-* grid is carried unchanged and is under its heaviest pressure in the chunk: ***mendicitas*** → "mendicancy" · ***mendicatio*** → "begging" · ***mendicare*** → "to beg" · ***mendicus*** → "beggar". p. 153's *si **mendicare** peccare est, **mendicitas** sive **mendicatio** peccatum est* sets three of the four in one clause and the English keeps them apart.
- ***vilificare se*** → "to make oneself vile" and ***vilificatio*** → "the making of oneself vile", kept apart from ***humiliare*** → "to humble" and ***abiectio*** → "abjection" — `q1`'s choice, and p. 151's censura III is the sharpest control the work has produced, since the adversary's whole objection turns on *se ipsum vilificare et humiliare* against *humiliare se mendicando.*
- ***petere*** → "to request" · ***rogare*** → "to ask" · ***quaerere*** → "to seek" · ***postulare*** → "to petition" · *accipere / recipere* → "receive". Load-bearing again at p. 155, whose three paragraphs divide *eleemosynarum acceptionem* from *eleemosynarum petitionem.*
- ***auctoritas / subauctoritas*** → "authority / sub-authority" · ***potestas*** → "power" · ***plenitudo potestatis*** → "fulness of power" · ***praelatus*** → "prelate" · ***procurationes*** → "procurations" · ***opitulationes*** → "helps" (I Cor. 12:28, the Douay's own word) · ***otiositas*** → "idleness" (NOT a *vaca-* word) · ***impertinentia / inconsequentia / dubia*** → "impertinent / inconsequent / doubtful", the five censurae's own names, kept fixed across heading and body.
- **Scripture** on the Douay base, adjusted where Quaracchi's Latin differs: Ps. 39:18 *Ego sum mendicus et pauper* → "I am a beggar and poor" (Douay "beggar" retained, so that *mendicus* keeps the register's word); Ps. 33:11's *egenus* → "needy"; I Cor. 12:28's *opitulationes* → "helps"; Phil. 2:10 *omne genu flectitur* rendered in the indicative, as Quaracchi prints it.

### ⭐ PRINTING ANOMALIES — AS PRINTED, NOT EMENDED (pp. 148–155)

- ⭐ **p. 153 — the marginale beside section `V.` prints `Censura quarta.`, repeating the marginale beside `IV.` on p. 152.** Read at 6× on a full-width strip: the two lines are `Censura` / `quarta.` and there is no trace of a `quinta`. The body is unambiguous (`V. Refellere etiam conatur plurima…`, the fifth of the five censurae the opener names), so this is a compositor's slip in the gloss, not in the text. **Recorded as printed; the gloss is NOT corrected in the Marginalia list below.**
- **p. 152 arg. — *…et Benedictus, qui humillime suscipiebat*¹ *Nusquam autem inveniens…*** — the anchor stands where the sentence's full stop should be and **no point is printed.** Read at 4×: the superscript `1` sits on the shoulder of the final `t` and the baseline after it is clean paper. The `bon-qmt-q1-a1` argument-21 precedent (a numeral printed without its following point) governs; **reproduced as printed, not supplied.**
- ⭐⭐ **NUMERAL SIGLA FOR THE PRINTED EDITIONS ARE A REAL FEATURE OF THIS WORK'S APPARATUS, not a misprint.** p. 150 n. 2 reads `M et 5 sicut rabies mihi imponit`, and the bare `5` reads as a digit at 10× (flat top bar, lower bowl; it is not a failed `E`). It was doubted until **p. 152 n. 5 (`Ex D et 1, 2, 3 supplevimus ad`) and p. 152 n. 9 (`et cum 1, 2, 3, 4 ut vadat`) and p. 154 n. 3 (`M et 1, 2, 3, 4 cum textu originali`) and p. 154 n. 5 (`M 1, 2, 3, 4 qua repudiabant`) settled it**: Quaracchi sigles the printed editions of this work by arabic numerals alongside the letter sigla for the codices. **Transcribed as printed everywhere, and expanded in the English only** ("edition 5", "editions 1, 2, 3"). ⚠ **Do not read a bare numeral in this work's apparatus as a damaged letter.**
- **p. 148's first marginale prints `Determinator.`** — set as `Determina-` / `tor.` and read on a full-image-width strip; the expected gloss here would be *Determinatur.* **Recorded as printed, not normalised** (the `bon-praec-c2` `Duplex no-` precedent: a marginale is transcribed as the compositor set it).
- **p. 153 carries the signature line `S. Bonav. — Tom. V.` under its LEFT register and the gathering signature `20` under its RIGHT** — the same class as p. 137's and p. 145's, and like them not apparatus.

### ▶ SPAN AND BOUNDARY 3 — FIXED POSITIVELY ON THE PLATE

⭐⭐ **The articulus closes on p. 155, and p. 156 is a LEAF EDGE, split 0/5.** `raw/vision/vol5/p-156.png` was extracted **ALONE and read first**, per the standing order, before pp. 148–155 were pulled: **`ARTICULUS III.` stands at the very head of p. 156**, full measure, with its subtitle *Utrum pauperes validi, et maxime regulares, ad opera manualia universaliter sint astricti* and its own numbered opener *Tertio ad maiorem evidentiam…* on the same leaf. Applying the **p. 498 rule** — *a shared leaf forwards a register entry only where the incoming unit's numbered BODY carries its anchor* — the leaf is not shared at all: Articulus II's body ends *Amen.* on p. 155, **all five of p. 156's register entries anchor in Articulus III's body**, and `bon-qpe-q2-a2` therefore owns nothing whatever of p. 156. ⛔ **The raw's `ARTICULUS III.` at L32212 sits six lines below p. 156's own numeral, which the OCR prints `136` — the worn-`5`-reads-as-`3` class — so the raw alone would have put the heading ambiguously; the plate settled it.** ▶ **`bon-qpe-q2-a3` opens clean at p. 156 and inherits NOTHING.**

**170 apparatus entries over pp. 134–155**, the largest register any Vol V chunk has carried. **Zero `[?]` flags.** **Fourteen runovers — twelve gutter-crossing, two page-crossing** (pp. 136 n. 8 → 137 and 138 n. 10 → 139); the single ledger line appended to `manual-review/vol5-runover-ledger.tsv` is **comma-separated**, per the standing warning that the checker swallows a semicolon line silently. **One hundred and three marginalia, none clipped.** **Three translator's notes**, the corpus's first chunk to carry more than one.

### ▶ OPEN QUESTIONS — FOR THE WORK-CLOSE GATE

1. ⭐⭐⭐ **THE *ADDITAMENTUM* IS NOT TRANSCRIBED IN THIS CHUNK, AND THE DECISION IS WILSON'S.** p. 155 n. 8 (transcribed in full above) records that the editions carry, after the articulus, an **additamentum of nine authorities** which Quaracchi judge *non genuinum* and *ad perfectionem quaestionis superfluum* and have relegated *ad calcem.* **Measured on the plates:** it begins on p. 155, in the right footer block, below n. 8 and under its own marginale `Additamentum.` (*Hanc etiam eandem veritatem multiplex astruit auctoritas non solum ipsius Hieronymi, verum etiam Ioannis Chrysostomi et Bernardi…*), continues in **p. 156's LEFT footer block** (Chrysostom hom. 88 and hom. 69, then Bernard/Aelred), and **breaks off mid-clause at the foot of p. 156** — *…quaerens, ubi Christus fuit in triduo, dicit sic: « Per* — with blank paper beneath it. **pp. 157 and 158 carry no additamentum block at all**, so it does not resume on the next two leaves. It therefore straddles the Articulus II / Articulus III boundary, sits beneath another unit's body, and does not close within reach of this chunk. **Three reasons it was left out rather than guessed at:** (a) the editors say they relegated it *ad calcem,* which is not this articulus's foot; (b) a span of 134–155 cannot own matter printed on p. 156 and later; (c) the corpus's one precedent — `bon-hex-c23`'s p. 449 Additamentum, made an anchored apparatus entry — was **Wilson's own ruling**, taken on a case that closed on the leaf it opened on, and it does not decide this one. ▶ **Nothing is lost to the reader in the meantime: p. 155 n. 8 states the addition's existence, extent, contents and the editors' reasoning verbatim.** ⛔ **`bon-qpe-q2-a3` must read p. 156's left footer block and say where the additamentum goes; the work-close gate should settle it for the work.**
2. **The codex-`I` question, unchanged from the earlier spans** — and pp. 148–155 add eight more `I` citations (p. 148 n. 1; p. 149 n. 3; p. 150 nn. 2, 8; p. 151 nn. 6, 8; p. 152 nn. 6, 7, 8, 9; p. 153 n. 5; p. 154 nn. 6, 7, 9), so the siglum is used steadily over the whole articulus, which strengthens the case that it is a real siglum and not a broken `L`.
3. **p. 139 n. 8's missing initial** and **p. 146 n. 5's *diligentia*** (recorded above), unchanged.
4. **Quaracchi's own internal `fundam. N` back-references into `bon-qpe-q2-a1`'s `Sᴇᴅ ᴄᴏɴᴛʀᴀ`** — nine sites in this chunk. The resolver will not parse a bare `fundam. N`, so none of them produces a citation record; as at `q1` and `q2-a1`, this is a resolver matter for the gate, not an edit.
5. **Resolver artefacts named for the gate:** p. 148 n. 3's `supra pag. 140, nota 5`, p. 150 n. 2's `In solut. ad 20.`, p. 151 n. 3's `supra pag. 126, nota 2`, p. 153 n. 1's `Art. 1. in corp. et hic ad 11`, p. 155 n. 6's `supra pag. 130, nota 1. 2.` — all INTERNAL to `bon-qpe-q1`/`q2-a1`/this chunk and all correct as printed, but a bare `pag. N` inherits the *volume* rather than the note's own last-named tome, the known `q5-a2` class.
6. **The numeral edition-sigla** (`1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`) are new to the corpus in this chunk and are recorded above; if any later chunk meets a bare numeral in a variant clause, it is an edition siglum, not a damaged letter.
7. ⚠⚠ **BUILDING THIS CHUNK CHANGED WHAT TWO OTHER CHUNKS' CITATIONS RESOLVE TO, AND AT LEAST ONE OF THE NEW RESOLUTIONS IS WRONG.** The `citations.tsv` diff shows three inbound lines that now land here: `bon-hex-c7`'s `supra pag. 149, nota 8` (and the `ibid.` that follows it by anaphora) and `bon-don-c8`'s bare `pag. 154`. ⛔ **p. 149 carries SIX notes, so `nota 8` cannot be this leaf** — the reference is to another tome, and the resolver has inherited the *volume* rather than the note's own last-named tome, the `bon-qmt-q5-a2` class; it now *looks* resolved, which is the `bon-qmt-q6-a2` warning firing again. `bon-don-c8`'s `pag. 154` is the same shape and has no arithmetic check to catch it. **Both for the work-close gate; no text was edited on either side.** ▶ The general lesson stands: **read the citations.tsv diff for INBOUND lines after building a chunk, not only for the chunk's own records.**
